5 Faithful English translation

10.1.1 Greater than the great — on the far shore of the waters, in the middle of the world, on the back of the vault of heaven — having fully entered the lights with [his] brightness, Prajāpati moves within the womb. In which all this comes together and goes apart, in which all the gods have settled: that indeed is what has been, that also what is to be, ah, this [too]; that [rests] in the imperishable, in the highest space. By which space and heaven and earth are covered, by which the sun shines with heat and radiance; whom the seers weave within the ocean; which [is] in the imperishable, the highest — [it brings forth] the creatures. From which the bringing forth of the world came; [which] with water let loose living beings on the earth; which by the plants entered men and animals, the beings moving and unmoving. Than this nothing else is higher or subtler; which [is] higher than the high, greater than the great; which [is] one, unmanifest, of endless form, all, ancient, beyond darkness.

10.1.2 That indeed they call right order, that also truth; that indeed [is] brahman, the highest of the seers. The navel of the world bears sacrifice and public works, the world born in many ways and being born. That indeed [is] fire, that wind, that the sun, that also the moon; that indeed [is] the bright, the immortal; that brahman, that the waters, he Prajāpati. All the blinks were born from the lightning-like Person; kalās, muhūrtas, kāṣṭhās, days and nights, all together; half-months, months, seasons and the year — let them be arranged. He milked out the waters, these two, the mid-region and heaven. No one has grasped him above, nor across, nor in the middle; no one rules over him; his name [is] great glory.

10.1.3 His form does not stand within sight; no one sees him with the eye. Grasped by the heart, by the master of thought, by the mind — those who know him become immortal. ‘From the waters born’; ‘the golden embryo’ — [these] eight. This god indeed [is in] all the quarters; first born indeed, he also [is] within the womb. He, being born; he, to be born; facing inward he stands, facing every way: with eyes everywhere and faces everywhere, hands everywhere and feet everywhere. With his two arms he welds together, with wings together, producing heaven and earth, the one god. Vena, seeing that, knowing all the worlds — where all becomes one nest; in which this [comes] together and apart, the one; he, woven along and across, pervading creatures — the gandharva by name, knowing, proclaimed that immortal, hidden in the caves.

10.1.4 Three steps are placed in the caves; who knows that becomes the father of the begetter. He [is] our kinsman, the begetter, he the ordainer; he knows the abodes, all the worlds — where the gods, having attained immortality, reached the abodes in the third [world]. They go around heaven and earth at once, around the worlds, around the quarters, around heaven. Having unravelled the stretched thread of right order, he saw that, he became that among creatures. Having gone round the worlds, round the beings, round all the quarters and directions, Prajāpati, the first-born of right order, by himself became one with the self. The wondrous lord of the seat, dear to Indra, desirable, gain [and] intelligence I have obtained. Blaze up, Jātavedas, striking away my destruction.

10.1.5 Bring me cattle and livelihood; grant me places to dwell. Do not harm, Jātavedas, our cow, horse, man, household. Not holding [our faults], Agni, come; surround me with prosperity. We know the thousand-eyed Person; let us meditate on the great god; may that Rudra impel us. We know that Person; let us meditate on the great god; may that Rudra impel us. We know that Person; let us meditate on the one with the curved trunk; may that tusked one impel us. We know that Person; let us meditate on the one with the wheel-like mouth;

10.1.6 may that Nandin impel us. We know that Person; let us meditate on the one with the great army; may that six-faced one impel us. We know that Person; let us meditate on the golden-winged one; may that Garuḍa impel us. We know the one whose self is the Veda; let us meditate on the golden embryo; may that brahman impel us. We know Nārāyaṇa; let us meditate on Vāsudeva; may that Viṣṇu impel us. We know the one with adamantine claws; let us meditate on the sharp-fanged one;

10.1.7 may that Man-Lion impel us. We know the maker of light; let us meditate on the maker of great brilliance; may that Āditya impel us. We know Vaiśvānara; let us meditate on the playful one (?); may that Agni impel us. We know [the daughter] of Kātyāyana; let us meditate, O maiden Kumārī; may that Durgā impel us. Higher than a thousand, the goddess, hundred-rooted, hundred-shooted — may dūrvā, destroyer of bad dreams, take away all my evil, sprouting from joint after joint, from knot after knot, all round.

10.1.8 So multiply us, dūrvā, by a thousand and a hundred — you who spread out by a hundred and sprout by a thousand: to that one, to you, goddess Iṣṭakā, may we offer with an offering. O [earth] trodden by horses, trodden by chariots, trodden by Viṣṇu, bearer of wealth: I shall bear you on my head; protect me at every step. Earth, a cow, the bearer, the bearer of worlds — you were lifted up by the black hundred-armed Boar. O earth, destroy my sin, the wrong done by me. O earth, you are given by Brahmā, consecrated by Kāśyapa. O earth, give me nourishment; in you all is established.

10.1.9 O earth, O firm ground, drive out all that [sin] of mine, O earth. By that [sin] being slain I go to the highest goal. From what we fear, Indra, from that make safety for us. Bountiful one, help — that [power] of yours for our protection; strike down the haters, the assailants. Giver of well-being, lord of the people, slayer of Vṛtra, subduer of assailants, the bull Indra — may he go before us, giver of well-being, maker of safety. Well-being for us — Indra of increased fame; well-being for us — Pūṣan who possesses all; well-being for us — Tārkṣya with unharmed wheel-rim; well-being for us may Bṛhaspati grant. Of quick anger, swift in striking, roaring, vigorous, armed with arrows, possessing the soma-dregs, Soma [crushed] all the brushwood and trees; the counterparts on this side did not harm Indra.

10.1.10 Brahman, born first in the east, the lovely one, uncovered the bright [worlds] from the boundary; he uncovered the deep ones, the near ones, the abodes of this [world], the source of the existent and of the non-existent. Be kind, O earth, bringer forth of men, giver of dwelling; grant us shelter, [you who are] broad. Her whose sign is fragrance, hard to assail, ever nourished, rich in cattle, mistress of all beings — that Śrī I call here. May Śrī come to me; may my misfortune perish. The gods led by Viṣṇu indeed won these worlds with the metres, so that they could not be won back. May the great Indra, thunderbolt-armed, of sixteen parts, grant shelter.

10.1.11 May the bountiful one make well-being for us; may he strike the evil that hates us. Make [me] a presser of soma, one who sounds [the hymns], O lord of brahman — [as] Kakṣīvant, who [was] Uśij’s son. The body, defilement of the sacrifice, [is] a base place: in it may he sink who hates us. The foot [is] a purifier, outstretched, ancient, purified by which one crosses wrongdoings: purified by that pure purifier, may we cross over evil, the enemy. In accord, Indra, with your troop, with the Maruts, drink the soma, slayer of Vṛtra, hero, knowing; strike the enemies, push away the attackers; then make safety for us on all sides. May the waters and plants be good friends to us; may they be bad friends to him who hates us and whom we hate. Waters, you are bringers of joy: set us towards strength.

10.1.12 For the great, for delight, for seeing. That most auspicious essence of yours — give us a share of it here, like loving mothers. For that let us go readily to you, for whose dwelling you quicken [us]; and, waters, make us generate. I approach golden-horned Varuṇa for refuge; give me a ford, being asked. What I have eaten of the bad, and acceptance from sinners, what wrong I have done by mind, speech or deed — that of ours may Indra, Varuṇa, Bṛhaspati and Savitṛ purify again and again. Homage to Agni who dwells in the waters, homage to Indra, homage to Varuṇa, homage to Vāruṇī, homage to the waters.

10.1.13 Whatever of the waters is harmful, whatever impure, whatever unsettling — let that go away. From eating too much, from drinking too much, and what [comes] from accepting from the fierce — that of ours may Varuṇa the king wipe away with his hand. I, then, sinless, stainless, released, freed of guilt, having climbed the summit of heaven, would go to sharing a world with Brahman. And Varuṇa who is in the waters — may he, the wiper-away of sin, purify. Attend to this praise of mine, Gaṅgā, Yamunā, Sarasvatī, Śutudrī, with Paruṣṇī; hear, Marudvṛdhā with Asiknī, Ārjīkīyā with Vitastā and Suṣomā. Right order and truth were born from the kindled heat; from that night was born, from that the ocean, the flood.

10.1.14 From the ocean, from the flood, the year was born, ordaining days and nights, master of all that blinks. The creator fashioned sun and moon as before, and heaven and earth, and the mid-region, and also the world of light. Whatever stain of our own [is] on earth, in the mid-region, in heaven and earth — may Varuṇa, the waters, the remover of sin, purify these [of] that. May the Vasus purify, may Varuṇa purify, may Aghamarṣaṇa purify. He in the midst of what has been, the guardian of the world; he [gives] the worlds of the doers of good; he [gives] the golden [world] of death; the golden heaven resting between heaven and earth —

10.1.15 — that heaven grant to us. The moist blazes; I am the light. The light blazes; I am brahman. I who am — I am brahman. I am — I am brahman. I myself, I offer myself: svāhā. One who does what must not be done, who has broken his vow, a thief, a slayer of an embryo, a violator of the teacher’s bed — [by] Varuṇa of the waters, the remover of sin, [he] is released from that sin. ‘I am a ground of impurity; do [not] make me weep’ — so the wise declare. The ocean has strode over [all], bringing forth creatures at the first dispensation, the king of the world. The bull, on the filter, on the woollen ridge, has grown greatly — Soma, the drop, being pressed.

Word for word; words supplied for English are in [brackets].

6 Readable English an interpretive rendering, not a literal translation

10.1.1 Greater than the greatest things — beyond the farthest shore of the ocean, at the centre of the world, above the vault of heaven — the lord of creatures moves within the cosmic womb, having entered every light with his own. In him all this gathers and disperses; in him all the gods are seated. He is what has been and what is to be, and this present world too, resting in the imperishable, the highest space. He covers sky, heaven and earth; by him the sun burns and shines; the seers weave him through the ocean of the world. From him came the birth of the world; by water he set living beings on the earth, and as food he entered men, animals and all that moves and does not move. Nothing is higher or subtler than he: higher than the high, greater than the great, one, unmanifest, of endless form, the whole, the ancient, beyond darkness.

10.1.2 They call it right order and they call it truth; it is brahman, the highest thing the seers know. As the hub of the world it bears the whole world, born in so many ways and still being born, and all its sacrifices and good works. It is fire, wind, sun and moon; it is the shining and the immortal; it is brahman, the waters and the lord of creatures. From that shining Person every instant of time was born — the smallest moments, the hours, days and nights, fortnights, months, seasons and the year. He drew sustenance from the waters and from both regions above. No one has ever grasped him — not above, not across, not in the middle. No one rules him. His very name is great glory.

10.1.3 His form is not within sight; no eye has ever seen him. He is grasped by the heart, by the mind turned inward; those who know him become immortal. [Here the recitation adds two passages known by their opening words: the anuvāka ‘born from the waters’ and the eight verses of the hymn to the Golden Embryo.] This god is in every quarter. He was born first, and he is within the womb; he is what is being born and what will be born; he stands within all and faces every way, with eyes, faces, hands and feet on every side. The one god brings forth heaven and earth, welding them together with his arms and wings. The gandharva Vena saw that in which the whole world becomes a single nest, in which all this comes together and comes apart, the one that is woven through creatures lengthwise and across; knowing all the worlds, he proclaimed that immortal thing hidden in the cave of the heart.

10.1.4 Three states lie hidden in the cave of the heart; whoever knows them becomes father even to his own father. He is our kinsman and our begetter; he orders all things and knows every abode and every world, where the gods, having won immortality, reached their places in the third heaven. Those who know go at once around heaven and earth, around the worlds, the directions and the sky. One who has unwound the long thread of truth and seen it becomes that, among all creatures. Prajāpati, first-born of truth, went round the worlds, the beings and all the directions, and by his own self became one with the self. I have come to the wondrous lord of the seat, dear to Indra, longed for by all; I have gained his gifts and intelligence. Blaze up, Agni, and strike away my ruin.

10.1.5 Bring me cattle and long life, and grant me places to live in. Do not harm, Agni, our cows, horses, people or homes. Come to us without holding our faults against us, and surround me with prosperity. [The gāyatrīs of the deities begin:] We know the thousand-eyed Person and meditate on the great god — may Rudra inspire us. We know that Person and meditate on the great god — may Rudra inspire us. We know that Person and meditate on the one with the curved trunk — may the tusked one inspire us. We know that Person and meditate on the one whose mouth is like a discus —

10.1.6 — may Nandin inspire us. We know that Person and meditate on the commander of the great army — may the six-faced one inspire us. We know that Person and meditate on the golden-winged one — may Garuḍa inspire us. We know him whose self is the Veda and meditate on the golden embryo — may Brahman inspire us. We know Nārāyaṇa and meditate on Vāsudeva — may Viṣṇu inspire us. We know the one with claws like adamant and meditate on the sharp-fanged one —

10.1.7 — may the Man-Lion inspire us. We know the maker of light and meditate on the giver of great splendour — may the Sun inspire us. We know Vaiśvānara and meditate on the playful one — may Agni inspire us. We know Kātyāyana’s daughter and meditate on you, maiden Kumārī — may Durgā inspire us. [The mantras of the ritual bath begin, said over dūrvā grass:] May dūrvā, the shining one, worth more than a thousand purifiers, with a hundred roots and a hundred shoots, the destroyer of bad dreams, take away all my wrongdoing — dūrvā that sprouts from every joint and every knot.

10.1.8 As you spread by hundreds and grow by thousands, dūrvā, so make us spread by hundreds and thousands. Goddess, we honour you with an offering. [Over the earth taken for the bath:] Earth, walked on by horses, by chariots and by Viṣṇu’s stride, bearer of all wealth — I shall carry you on my head; protect me at every step. You are the earth, a giving cow, the bearer of all the worlds, lifted out of the flood by the great black Boar of a hundred arms. Earth, destroy the wrong I have done. You were given by Brahmā and consecrated by Kāśyapa. Earth, give me strength; in you everything is established.

10.1.9 Earth, firm ground of all beings, drive out all my wrongdoing; with that wrong destroyed, I go to the highest goal. Indra, give us safety from whatever we fear. Bountiful one, use your power to protect us; strike down those who hate and attack us. May Indra go before us — giver of well-being, lord of the people, slayer of Vṛtra, master of all assailants, the rain-giving bull, maker of safety. May Indra of great fame, Pūṣan who owns all things, Garuḍa whose course nothing harms, and Bṛhaspati give us well-being. [A verse of the Ṛgveda:] Fierce, swift to strike, roaring, mighty, armed and strong with soma, Soma swept through the forests; no rival on earth has ever matched Indra.

10.1.10 Brahman, born first in the east and longed for by all, opened up the shining worlds from their bounds, and laid open the deep quarters near at hand, the dwellings of this world, the source of what is and of what is not. Be kind to us, Earth; you bring forth people and give them a place to live; give us shelter, wide one. [Over the earth taken for the bath:] I call here Śrī, known by her fragrance, unshakeable, ever-nourished, rich in cattle, mistress of all beings. May fortune come to me; may misfortune leave me. The gods, led by Viṣṇu, won these worlds with the metres, never to be won back. May great Indra, thunderbolt in hand, full like the sixteen-digit moon, give us shelter.

10.1.11 May Indra give us well-being and strike down the evil that hates us. Lord of the Veda, make me one who presses soma and sounds the hymns, as you made Kakṣīvant, son of Uśij. Let the one who hates us sink into what is base. [Standing knee-deep in the water:] Viṣṇu’s foot is a purifier, outstretched and ancient; purified by it a man crosses beyond his wrongs — purified by that pure purifier, may we cross beyond evil, our enemy. Indra, slayer of Vṛtra, hero, come with the Maruts and drink the soma; strike our enemies, drive off our attackers and give us safety on every side. [Standing navel-deep:] May the waters and the plants be friends to us, and unfriendly to whoever hates us and whom we hate. Waters, you bring joy: give us strength.

10.1.12 — for greatness, for joy, for the vision [of the highest]. Like loving mothers, give us here a share of your most blessed essence. For that we come gladly to you, waters, who are pleased to destroy our wrongs; make us fruitful. I take refuge in Varuṇa, whose crown is golden; you whom I ask, give me a place to bathe. Whatever I have eaten in the houses of the wicked, whatever gifts I have taken from sinners, whatever wrong I have done in thought, word or deed — may Indra, Varuṇa, Bṛhaspati and Savitṛ purify it, again and again. Homage to Agni hidden in the waters, to Indra, to Varuṇa, to Varuṇa’s consort, and to the waters.

10.1.13 Let whatever in the waters is harmful, unclean or disturbing go away. Whatever wrong comes of eating or drinking too much, or of taking gifts from dangerous people, may King Varuṇa wipe it away with his own hand. Then, sinless, stainless, set free and rid of guilt, I would climb to the summit of heaven and reach the world of Brahman. May Varuṇa, who dwells in the waters, the remover of sin, purify me. [The rivers invoked:] Gaṅgā, Yamunā, Sarasvatī, Śutudrī, Paruṣṇī; Asiknī, Marudvṛdhā, Vitastā, Ārjīkīyā, Suṣomā — come and hear my praise. [The aghamarṣaṇa, said under the water:] From the blazing heat of reflection right order and truth were born; from it the night, from it the billowing ocean.

10.1.14 From the ocean was born the year, which orders days and nights and rules all that lives. The creator made the sun and moon as before, and heaven, earth, the mid-region and the world of light. [After the aghamarṣaṇa, while bathing:] Whatever stain is ours, on earth, in the mid-region or between heaven and earth, may Varuṇa, who is the waters and removes sin, wash it from us. May the Vasus purify us; may Varuṇa purify us; may Aghamarṣaṇa purify us. He is the guardian of all that has been and of the world; he grants the worlds of those who do good; he holds the golden world of death; and the golden heaven that rests between heaven and earth —

10.1.15 — grant us that heaven. [Sipping water after the bath:] This water shines; I am that light. The light shines; I am brahman. I who am — I am brahman. I am — I am brahman. I offer myself, in myself: svāhā. Whoever has done what is forbidden, broken his vow, stolen, killed, or violated his teacher’s bed is freed from that sin by Varuṇa, lord of the waters, the remover of wrong. [For hidden wrongs:] ‘I am a ground of impurity — do not make me weep,’ the wise say. The ocean has filled all things, the king of the world, bringing forth creatures at the first ordering of things. The bull has grown great on the woollen filter, on the summit — Soma, the pressed drop.

Key concepts ब्रह्मन् Brahman अव्यक्त avyakta, the unmanifest ऋत the right; cosmic and moral order सत्य truth, the real हिरण्यगर्भ Hiraṇyagarbha, ‘golden embryo’ गुहा the cave गन्धर्व gandharva, celestial musician गायत्री gāyatrī प्राणायाम prāṇāyāma, breath control मन्त्र mantra

7 Explain it to a beginner

This is the longest section of the Mahānārāyaṇa, and it has three parts. The first is a hymn about the one who lies beyond the ocean of the world and yet moves within it: all the gods rest in him, time itself is born from him, and no eye can see him, only the heart. Those who know him, the text says, become immortal. The second part is a row of short prayers in the same shape as the famous gāyatrī verse: ‘we know this god, we meditate on him, may he move us’, addressed in turn to Rudra, Gaṇeśa, Nandin, Skanda, Garuḍa, Brahmā, Viṣṇu, Narasiṃha, the Sun, Agni and Durgā. The third part is the set of mantras the old commentators assign to the ritual bath: words said over grass and earth, while standing in the water, while under it, and while sipping water afterwards. It ends with the bather saying, ‘I am light; I am brahman.’

An analogy Like a river that rises in the mountains and reaches the bathing steps of a village: the same water is first described from far off as the source of everything, then called by many local names, then simply stepped into.

8 Traditional philosophical commentary
Direct Textual Meaning

What does the anuvāka contain?

Three groups of mantras. 10.1.1–10.1.4: a hymn to the one ‘beyond the waters’, greater than the great and subtler than the subtle, in whom the world gathers and disperses, from whom time is born, whom no one has grasped and whom only the heart sees; two pratīkas point to passages recited at that place. 10.1.4–10.1.5: prayers to Agni and to the lord of the seat. 10.1.5–10.1.7: twelve gāyatrīs, to Rudra (twice), the tusked one, Nandin, the six-faced one, Garuḍa, Brahman, Viṣṇu, Narasiṃha, Āditya, Agni and Durgā. 10.1.7–10.1.15: mantras over dūrvā grass and earth, to Indra and the waters, to Varuṇa, to the rivers, the aghamarṣaṇa hymn ‘ṛtaṃ ca satyaṃ ca’, and the self-statements ‘I am brahman’.

Traditional Interpretation

How do the commentators divide it?

The anonymous commentator closes the first part with तदेवं ब्रह्मविद्या प्रतिपादिता, the second with एवं द्वादश गायत्र्यो व्याख्याताः, and opens the third with अथ स्नानाङ्गभूता मन्त्र उच्यन्ते. Sāyaṇa likewise says the knowledge of brahman is taught here from ambhasy apāre onward: एवमत्राप्यम्भस्य पार इत्यादिना ब्रह्मतत्त्वमभिहितम्.

Traditional Interpretation

What do the pratīkas in 10.1.3 point to?

The line adbhyas sambhūto hiraṇyagarbha ityaṣṭau names two passages by their opening words. The anonymous commentator says they are quoted from elsewhere to confirm what was just said (उक्तार्थदार्ढ्याय प्रदेशान्तरपठितान्मन्त्रानुदाहरति): the anuvāka beginning adbhyaḥ sambhūtaḥ, in which the knower of the highest is said to be free of death (तस्मिन्ननुवाके तमेवं विद्वानमृत इह भवतीति परमात्मतत्त्वविदो मरणराहित्यमुक्तम्), and the eight verses beginning hiraṇyagarbhaḥ samavartatāgre, found in the fourth book, first chapter, of the Saṃhitā (संहितायाश्चतुर्थकाण्डे प्रथमप्रपाठके). The Ānandāśrama and Tirupati texts write both passages out in full; the base keeps the pratīka.

Traditional Interpretation

Who are the gāyatrīs addressed to?

The anonymous commentator counts twelve and names the deity of each; he begins with Rudra in his universal form (तत्राऽऽदौ विश्वरूपधरं रुद्रं प्रार्थयते), explains the tusked one as Vināyaka, the one with the discus-like mouth as Nandikeśvara, and the commander of the great army as the six-faced god (महती देवसेना यस्य तस्मै). Sāyaṇa, following the Drāviḍa text, explains six (तत्र षड्भिर्गायत्रीभिरात्मप्राप्तिद्वारभूता देवताविशेषाः प्रार्थ्यन्ते); Bhāskara counts five beginning with tatpuruṣāya (तत्पुरुषायेत्याद्याः पञ्च गायत्र्यः) and then further gāyatrīs for Vighneśvara and others (अथ प्रसङ्गाद् विघ्नेश्वराद्याराधनार्था गायत्र्य आम्नाताः). The number of gāyatrīs is a difference of recension.

Traditional Interpretation

How are the bath mantras used?

The anonymous commentator assigns each group to a step of the bath. The dūrvā verses are said while placing dūrvā grass with earth on the head (शिरसि मृत्तिकया सह दूर्वां धारयितुं दूर्वाभिमन्त्रणमन्त्रानाह); Bhāskara calls the earth verses the mantras for taking earth at the time of bathing (स्नानकाले मृद्ग्रहणमन्त्राः). Standing knee-deep (जानुदघ्ने जले प्रविश्य जप्यौ मन्त्रावाह), then navel-deep (नाभिद्वयसजले स्थित्वा जप्यान्मन्त्रानाह); the rivers are invoked as places of bathing (तीर्थभूतानां गङ्गादीनामावाहनमन्त्रमाह); the aghamarṣaṇa is said under water with breath restraint (जले निमग्नस्य प्राणायामार्थमघमर्षणसूक्तमाह); then three verses for immersion (अघमर्षणं कृत्वाऽत ऊर्ध्वमवगाहनार्था ऋचो दर्शयति); then the sipping after the bath (स्नातवतः पुरुषस्याऽऽचमनमन्त्रानाह); and a further bath for hidden wrongs (रहस्यपापाक्षयार्थे स्नानमन्त्रमाह). Bhāskara agrees on the order after the aghamarṣaṇa (अघमर्षणोत्तरकालम् उत्तराभिस्तिसृभिः स्नायात्). This is the commentators’ account of the rite; the text itself does not describe the actions.

Traditional Interpretation

What are ṛta and satya in the aghamarṣaṇa?

The anonymous commentator explains ṛta as the five elements and satya as the world they make, both ‘true until knowledge dawns’; he records another view: केचित्तु ऋतं यथार्थभाषणं सत्यं यथार्थं संकल्पनमिति व्याचक्षते, that ṛta is speaking truly and satya is intending truly, and objects that on that view the creation of the elements must be supplied.

Linguistic and Textual Analysis

What does pracodayāt mean?

A subjunctive (leṭ) form of pra-√cud, ‘may he impel’. Bhāskara: प्रचोदयात् प्रचोदयतु वर्तयतु लेट्याडागमः — the augment ā of the subjunctive, glossed by the imperative. The anonymous commentator notes that in all these gāyatrīs gender and case are freely interchanged (सर्वत्र गायत्रीमन्त्रेषु बहुलं लिङ्गादिव्यत्ययः), so tatpuruṣāya, a dative, is read as an accusative.

Comparative Vedānta

Why do the witnesses differ so much here?

Sāyaṇa warns that from this point the readings of the different regions differ greatly (इत ऊर्ध्वं तेषु तेषु देशेषु श्रुतिपाठा अत्यन्तविलक्षणाः) and says he follows the Drāviḍa text (द्राविडपाठस्याऽऽदृतत्वाद्वयमपि तमेवाऽऽदृत्य व्याख्यास्यामः). The anonymous commentator records three arrangements of the whole text, of 64, 74 and 80 anuvākas (तत्र द्राविडानां चतुःषष्ठ्यनुवाकक्रमपाठः). In this anuvāka the Ānandāśrama text opens with harih om, writes out the two pratīka passages, reads nandiḥ where the base has ṣaṇmukhaḥ in the line after mahāsenāya, and adds an index line which names the twelve gāyatrī deities in order, Nandin and Ṣaṇmukha both among them; the Tirupati text adds gāyatryāḥ, writes out the pratīkas, and omits kārya in akāryakārī and the final induḥ.

9 Comparative Vedānta
Comparative Vedānta

Advaita · the anonymous commentator; Sāyaṇa, on 10.1 (Sāyaṇa: his anuvāka 1)

From the school’s commentary on this anuvāka

Both commentators read the hymn as teaching that the world seen as many through ignorance is, seen by knowledge, brahman alone (यद्यज्जगदविद्यादृष्ट्या नानाविधं प्रतीयते तत्सर्वं विद्यादृष्ट्याऽखण्डैकरसं ब्रह्मैव). On the bather’s words ‘I am brahman’ they say nothing new is gained: individuality had only been superimposed on brahman through ignorance (वस्तुतो ब्रह्मण्येव मयि पूर्वमज्ञानाज्जीवत्वमारोपितमासीत्), न तु नूतनं किंचिद्ब्रह्मत्वमागतम्. The anonymous commentator also says the knower of brahman becomes brahman (ब्रह्मविद्ब्रह्मैव भवति).

Viśiṣṭādvaita

No position is given for this school here: the edition does not force one.

Dvaita

No position is given for this school here: the edition does not force one.

Where they disagree

The Advaita reading is stated by the two commentators present, both of whom are Advaitins (the anonymous commentator opens by honouring Śaṅkara, आचार्यं शंकरं भजे). No Viśiṣṭādvaita or Dvaita commentary on this text is in the edition’s sources; Raṅgarāmānuja’s commentary on the Mahānārāyaṇa is not available here. Bhāskara’s comment on this anuvāka, as far as it survives in the copy used, is grammatical and ritual and states no school position.

10 Advanced Sanskrit analysis
Linguistic and Textual Analysis

Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • 10.1.1 महतो mahato → महतः

    visarga after a before a voiced sound

    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o: mahataḥ before mahīyān → mahato.

  • 10.1.1 प्रजापतिश्चरति prajāpatiścarati → प्रजापतिः + चरति

    visarga before c

    The visarga becomes ś before c: prajāpatiḥ + carati → prajāpatiś carati.

  • 10.1.1 चैति caiti → च + एति

    vowel fusion (vṛddhi)

    a + e → ai: ca + eti → caiti.

  • 10.1.1 भव्यमा इदं bhavyamā idaṃ → भव्यम् + आ + इदम्

    final m before a vowel; ā left apart

    m joins the vowel (bhavyam ā); ā and i are not fused — a hiatus the text keeps (see sandhi exceptions).

  • 10.1.1 येनावृतं yenāvṛtaṃ → येन + आवृतम्

    vowel fusion

    a + ā → ā: yena + āvṛtam → yenāvṛtam.

  • 10.1.1 यमन्तस्समुद्रे yamantassamudre → यम् + अन्तः + समुद्रे

    final m before a vowel; visarga before s

    m joins the vowel; the visarga before s is written as a doubled s in this recitation text: antaḥ samudre → antas samudre.

  • 10.1.1 जीवान्व्यचसर्ज jīvānvyacasarja → जीवान् + व्यचसर्ज

    final n kept

    A final n stays before v and is written joined to the next word.

  • 10.1.1 पशूग्श्च paśūgśca → पशून् + च

    final n before c, Taittirīya spelling

    n before c becomes ṃś (paśūṃś ca); the Taittirīya recitation text writes this anusvāra before a sibilant as ग्: पशूग्श्च.

  • 10.1.1 नान्यदणीयसं nānyadaṇīyasaṃ → न + अन्यत् + अणीयसम्

    vowel fusion; t voiced before a vowel

    a + a → ā (nānyat); t becomes d before a vowel: anyat aṇīyasam → anyad aṇīyasam.

  • 10.1.2 तदेवर्तं tadevartaṃ → तत् + एव + ऋतम्

    t voiced; a + ṛ

    t becomes d before a vowel (tad eva); a + ṛ → ar (guṇa): eva + ṛtam → evartam.

  • 10.1.2 सत्यमाहुस्तदेव satyamāhustadeva → सत्यम् + आहुः + तत् + एव

    final m before a vowel; visarga before t

    m joins the vowel; the visarga becomes s before t: āhuḥ tat → āhus tat.

  • 10.1.2 तदापस्स tadāpassa → तत् + आपः + सः

    t voiced; visarga before s; saḥ before a consonant

    tad āpaḥ; āpaḥ before s is written with a doubled s in this text (āpas sa); saḥ drops its visarga before a consonant.

  • 10.1.2 पुरुषादधि puruṣādadhi → पुरुषात् + अधि

    t voiced before a vowel

    puruṣāt + adhi → puruṣād adhi.

  • 10.1.2 काष्ठाश्चाहोरात्राश्च kāṣṭhāścāhorātrāśca → काष्ठाः + च + अहोरात्राः + च

    visarga before c; vowel fusion

    āḥ before c becomes āś (kāṣṭhāś ca); a + a → ā (cāhorātrāḥ).

  • 10.1.2 नैनमूर्ध्वं nainamūrdhvaṃ → न + एनम् + ऊर्ध्वम्

    vowel fusion (vṛddhi); m before a vowel

    a + e → ai (naenam → nainam); m joins the vowel.

  • 10.1.2 तस्येशे tasyeśe → तस्य + ईशे

    vowel fusion (guṇa)

    a + ī → e: tasya + īśe → tasyeśe.

  • 10.1.2 महद्यशः mahadyaśaḥ → महत् + यशः

    t voiced before a voiced sound

    mahat + yaśaḥ → mahad yaśaḥ.

  • 10.1.3 कश्चनैनम् kaścanainam → कश्चन + एनम्

    vowel fusion (vṛddhi)

    a + e → ai: kaścana + enam → kaścanainam.

  • 10.1.3 मनसाऽभिक्लृप्तो manasā'bhiklṛpto → मनसा + अभिक्लृप्तः

    vowel fusion; aḥ before a voiced sound

    ā + a → ā, written with avagraha here; abhiklṛptaḥ before y becomes abhiklṛpto.

  • 10.1.3 य एनं ya enaṃ → ये + एनम्

    e before a vowel

    Final e becomes a (ay with the y dropped) before a vowel: ye + enam → ya enam.

  • 10.1.3 अद्भ्यस्सम्भूतो adbhyassambhūto → अद्भ्यः + सम्भूतः

    visarga before s

    The visarga before s is written as a doubled s in this text: adbhyaḥ sambhūtaḥ → adbhyas sambhūtaḥ.

  • 10.1.3 हिरण्यगर्भ इत्यष्टौ hiraṇyagarbha ityaṣṭau → हिरण्यगर्भः + इति + अष्टौ

    aḥ before a vowel; i + a

    -aḥ before a vowel other than a loses the visarga (hiraṇyagarbha iti); i + a → ya: ity aṣṭau.

  • 10.1.3 प्रदिशोऽनु pradiśo'nu → प्रदिशः + अनु

    aḥ before a

    -aḥ + a → -o ’: pradiśo ’nu (P. 6.1.109 (एङः पदान्तादति)).

  • 10.1.3 जातस्स उ jātassa u → जातः + सः + उ

    visarga before s; saḥ before a vowel

    jātaḥ before s → jātas sa; saḥ loses its visarga: sa u.

  • 10.1.3 प्रत्यङ्मुखास्तिष्ठति pratyaṅmukhāstiṣṭhati → प्रत्यङ्मुखाः + तिष्ठति

    visarga before t

    āḥ before t becomes ās: pratyaṅmukhās tiṣṭhati.

  • 10.1.3 पतत्रैर्द्यावापृथिवी patatrairdyāvāpṛthivī → पतत्रैः + द्यावापृथिवी

    visarga after ai before a voiced sound

    A visarga after a vowel other than a/ā becomes r before a voiced sound: patatraiḥ dyāvā- → patatrair dyāvā-.

  • 10.1.3 भवत्येकनीळम् bhavatyekanīḻam → भवति + एकनीळम्

    i before a vowel

    i + e → ye: bhavaty ekanīḷam.

  • 10.1.4 यस्तद्वेद yastadveda → यः + तत् + वेद

    visarga before t; t voiced

    yaḥ before t → yas tad; tat before v → tad veda.

  • 10.1.4 बन्धुर्जनिता bandhurjanitā → बन्धुः + जनिता

    visarga after u before a voiced sound

    -uḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -ur: bandhur janitā.

  • 10.1.4 अमृतमानशानास्तृतीये amṛtamānaśānāstṛtīye → अमृतम् + आनशानाः + तृतीये

    m before a vowel; visarga before t

    amṛtam ānaśānāḥ → amṛtam ānaśānās tṛtīye.

  • 10.1.4 धामान्यभ्यैरयन्त dhāmānyabhyairayanta → धामानि + अभ्यैरयन्त

    i before a vowel

    i + a → ya: dhāmāny abhyairayanta.

  • 10.1.4 तदपश्यत्तदभवत्प्रजासु tadapaśyattadabhavatprajāsu → तत् + अपश्यत् + तत् + अभवत् + प्रजासु

    t voiced before a vowel; t kept

    tad apaśyat; t stays before t (apaśyat tad) and p (abhavat prajāsu).

  • 10.1.4 ऋतस्यात्मनाऽऽत्मानमभिसम्बभूव ṛtasyātmanā''tmānamabhisambabhūva → ऋतस्य + आत्मना + आत्मानम् + अभिसम्बभूव

    vowel fusion

    a + ā → ā (ṛtasyātmanā); ā + ā → ā, written ाऽऽ; m joins the vowel.

  • 10.1.4 जातवेदोऽपघ्नन्निरृतिं jātavedo'paghnannirṛtiṃ → जातवेदः + अपघ्नन् + निरृतिम्

    aḥ before a; n before n

    jātavedaḥ + apa- → jātavedo ’pa- (P. 6.1.109 (एङः पदान्तादति)); n stays before n.

  • 10.1.5 पशूग्श्च paśūgśca → पशून् + च

    final n before c, Taittirīya spelling

    n before c becomes ṃś; the recitation text writes this anusvāra as ग्: पशूग्श्च.

  • 10.1.5 मह्यमावह mahyamāvaha → मह्यम् + आवह

    final m before a vowel

    m joins the vowel: mahyam āvaha.

  • 10.1.5 दिशो दिश diśo diśa → दिशः + दिश

    aḥ before a voiced sound

    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o: diśo diśa.

  • 10.1.5 हिंसीज्जातवेदो hiṃsījjātavedo → हिंसीत् + जातवेदः

    t before j; aḥ before a voiced sound

    t assimilates to j: hiṃsīj jātavedo; jātavedaḥ before g becomes jātavedo.

  • 10.1.5 अबिभ्रदग्न आगहि abibhradagna āgahi → अबिभ्रत् + अग्ने + आगहि

    t voiced; e before a vowel

    abibhrat + agne → abibhrad agne; agne before ā loses its y: agna āgahi.

  • 10.1.5 तन्नो रुद्रः tanno rudraḥ → तत् + नः + रुद्रः

    t before a nasal; aḥ before a voiced sound

    t becomes n before n (tan naḥ); naḥ before r becomes no.

  • 10.1.5 तत्पुरुषाय tatpuruṣāya → तत् + पुरुषाय

    t kept before p

    tat puruṣāya, written joined.

  • 10.1.6 तन्नष्षण्मुखः tannaṣṣaṇmukhaḥ → तत् + नः + षण्मुखः

    t before n; visarga before ṣ

    tan naḥ; the visarga before ṣ is written as a doubled ṣ in this text: naṣ ṣaṇmukhaḥ.

  • 10.1.6 तन्नो गरुडः tanno garuḍaḥ → तत् + नः + गरुडः

    aḥ before a voiced sound

    naḥ before g becomes no.

  • 10.1.7 तन्नो आदित्यः tanno ādityaḥ → तत् + नः + आदित्यः

    aḥ before ā, Vedic spelling

    naḥ before ā is printed नो and left unjoined (classical न आदित्यः); see the sandhi exceptions.

  • 10.1.7 महद्युतिकराय mahadyutikarāya → महत् + द्युति + कर

    t voiced inside the compound

    mahat + dyuti → mahad-dyuti.

  • 10.1.7 काण्डात्काण्डात्प्ररोहन्ती kāṇḍātkāṇḍātprarohantī → काण्डात् + काण्डात् + प्ररोहन्ती

    t kept before k and p

    The final t stays before voiceless consonants: kāṇḍāt kāṇḍāt prarohantī.

  • 10.1.7 परुषःपरुषः परि paruṣaḥparuṣaḥ pari → परुषः + परुषः + परि

    visarga before p

    The visarga stays before p.

  • 10.1.8 एवा नो evā no → एवा + नः

    aḥ before a voiced sound

    naḥ before d becomes no.

  • 10.1.8 तस्यास्ते tasyāste → तस्याः + ते

    visarga before t

    tasyāḥ before t becomes tasyās.

  • 10.1.8 देवीष्टके devīṣṭake → देवि + इष्टके

    vowel fusion

    i + i → ī: devi + iṣṭake → devīṣṭake.

  • 10.1.8 भूमिर्धेनुर्धरणी bhūmirdhenurdharaṇī → भूमिः + धेनुः + धरणी

    visarga after i/u before a voiced sound

    iḥ and uḥ become ir and ur before dh.

  • 10.1.8 उद्धृताऽसि uddhṛtā'si → उद्धृता + असि

    vowel fusion

    ā + a → ā, written with avagraha in this text.

  • 10.1.8 यन्मया yanmayā → यत् + मया

    t before a nasal

    t becomes n before m: yan mayā.

  • 10.1.8 ब्रह्मदत्ताऽऽसि brahmadattā''si → ब्रह्मदत्ता + असि

    vowel fusion

    ā + a → ā, written ाऽऽ.

  • 10.1.8 काश्यपेनाभिमन्त्रिता kāśyapenābhimantritā → काश्यपेन + अभिमन्त्रिता

    vowel fusion

    a + a → ā.

  • 10.1.9 तन्मे tanme → तत् + मे

    t before a nasal

    t becomes n before m.

  • 10.1.9 यत इन्द्र yata indra → यतः + इन्द्र

    aḥ before a vowel

    -aḥ before a vowel other than a loses the visarga: yata indra.

  • 10.1.9 ततो नो अभयं tato no abhayaṃ → ततः + नः + अभयम्

    aḥ before a voiced sound; Vedic hiatus

    tataḥ before n → tato; naḥ before a is printed no and the a kept (see the sandhi exceptions).

  • 10.1.9 मघवञ्छग्धि maghavañchagdhi → मघवन् + शग्धि

    n before ś

    n becomes ñ and ś becomes ch: maghavañ chagdhi.

  • 10.1.9 तन्न ऊतये tanna ūtaye → तत् + नः + ऊतये

    t before n; aḥ before a vowel

    tan naḥ; naḥ before ū → na ūtaye.

  • 10.1.9 वृषेन्द्रः vṛṣendraḥ → वृषा + इन्द्रः

    vowel fusion (guṇa)

    ā + i → e: vṛṣā + indraḥ → vṛṣendraḥ.

  • 10.1.9 नस्स्वस्तिदा अभयङ्करः nassvastidā abhayaṅkaraḥ → नः + स्वस्तिदाः + अभयङ्करः

    visarga before s; āḥ before a vowel

    naḥ before s is written nas s-; svastidāḥ before a loses the visarga: svastidā abhayaṅkaraḥ.

  • 10.1.9 तार्क्ष्यो अरिष्टनेमिस्स्वस्ति tārkṣyo ariṣṭanemissvasti → तार्क्ष्यः + अरिष्टनेमिः + स्वस्ति

    Vedic hiatus; visarga before s

    tārkṣyaḥ before a is printed tārkṣyo and the a kept (see the exceptions); ariṣṭanemiḥ before s → ariṣṭanemis s-.

  • 10.1.9 शिमीवाञ्छरुमां ऋजीषी śimīvāñcharumāṃ ṛjīṣī → शिमीवान् + शरुमान् + ऋजीषी

    n before ś; Vedic -ān before a vowel

    śimīvān + śarumān → śimīvāñ charumān; -ān before a vowel becomes -āṃ, P. 8.3.9 (दीर्घादटि समानपादे).

  • 10.1.9 नार्वागिन्द्रं nārvāgindraṃ → न + अर्वाक् + इन्द्रम्

    vowel fusion; k voiced

    na + arvāk → nārvāk; k becomes g before a vowel: arvāg indram.

  • 10.1.10 पुरस्ताद्वि purastādvi → पुरस्तात् + वि

    t voiced before a voiced sound

    purastāt + vi → purastād vi.

  • 10.1.10 सीमतस्सुरुचो वेन आवः sīmatassuruco vena āvaḥ → सीमतः + सुरुचः + वेनः + आवः

    visarga before s; aḥ before a voiced sound; aḥ before a vowel

    sīmataḥ before s → sīmatas s-; surucaḥ before v → suruco; venaḥ before ā → vena āvaḥ.

  • 10.1.10 विष्ठास्सतश्च viṣṭhāssataśca → विष्ठाः + सतः + च

    visarga before s and c

    viṣṭhāḥ → viṣṭhās s-; sataḥ before c → sataś ca.

  • 10.1.10 तामिहोपह्वये tāmihopahvaye → ताम् + इह + उपह्वये

    m before a vowel; a + u

    tām iha; a + u → o: ihopahvaye.

  • 10.1.10 देवाश्छन्दोभिरिमान् लोकान् devāśchandobhirimān lokān → देवाः + छन्दोभिः + इमान् + लोकान्

    visarga before ch; iḥ before a vowel

    devāḥ before ch → devāś; chandobhiḥ before i → chandobhir imān; imān before l is left as printed.

  • 10.1.10 महां इन्द्रो mahāṃ indro → महान् + इन्द्रः

    Vedic -ān before a vowel

    -ān before a vowel in the same pāda becomes -āṃ, P. 8.3.9 (दीर्घादटि समानपादे).

  • 10.1.11 योऽस्मान्द्वेष्टि yo'smāndveṣṭi → यः + अस्मान् + द्वेष्टि

    aḥ before a; n kept before d

    yaḥ + asmān → yo ’smān (P. 6.1.109 (एङः पदान्तादति)); asmān dveṣṭi written joined.

  • 10.1.11 य औशिजम् ya auśijam → यः + औशिजम्

    aḥ before a vowel

    -aḥ before a vowel other than a loses the visarga: ya auśijam.

  • 10.1.11 तस्मिन्त्सीदतु tasmintsīdatu → तस्मिन् + सीदतु

    n before s, t inserted

    A t may be inserted between final n and s: tasmint sīdatu, P. 8.3.30 (नश्च).

  • 10.1.11 पूतस्तरति pūtastarati → पूतः + तरति

    visarga before t

    pūtaḥ before t → pūtas tarati.

  • 10.1.11 वृत्रहञ्छूर vṛtrahañchūra → वृत्रहन् + शूर

    n before ś

    n becomes ñ and ś becomes ch: vṛtrahañ chūra.

  • 10.1.11 शत्रूंरप śatrūṃrapa → शत्रून् + अप

    Vedic -ūn before a vowel

    -n after a long vowel before a vowel becomes -ṃr, P. 8.3.9 (दीर्घादटि समानपादे); the commentator gives the rule: दीर्घादटि समानपादे.

  • 10.1.11 नुदस्वाथाभयं nudasvāthābhayaṃ → नुदस्व + अथ + अभयम्

    vowel fusion

    a + a → ā twice: nudasvātha, athābhayam.

  • 10.1.11 ओषधयस्सन्तु oṣadhayassantu → ओषधयः + सन्तु

    visarga before s

    Written with a doubled s in this text.

  • 10.1.11 भूयासुऱ्योऽस्मान्द्वेष्टि bhūyāsuऱ्yo'smāndveṣṭi → भूयासुः + यः + अस्मान् + द्वेष्टि

    uḥ before a voiced sound

    -uḥ before y becomes -ur; the recitation text writes this r with the dotted ऱ; yaḥ + asmān → yo ’smān; asmān dveṣṭi.

  • 10.1.11 आपो हि ष्ठा āpo hi ṣṭhā → आपः + हि + ष्ठा

    aḥ before h; s retroflexed after i

    āpaḥ before h → āpo; after hi the s of stha becomes ṣ (Vedic), and the vowel is lengthened.

  • 10.1.12 यो वः yo vaḥ → यः + वः

    aḥ before a voiced sound

    yaḥ before v → yo.

  • 10.1.12 रसस्तस्य rasastasya → रसः + तस्य

    visarga before t

    rasaḥ before t → rasas tasya.

  • 10.1.12 भाजयतेह bhājayateha → भाजयत + इह

    vowel fusion (guṇa)

    a + i → e: bhājayata + iha → bhājayateha.

  • 10.1.12 उशतीरिव uśatīriva → उशतीः + इव

    visarga after ī before a vowel

    -īḥ before a vowel becomes -īr: uśatīr iva.

  • 10.1.12 तस्मा अरं tasmā araṃ → तस्मै + अरम्

    ai before a vowel

    ai before a vowel becomes ā (āy with the y dropped): tasmā aram.

  • 10.1.12 पापेभ्यश्च pāpebhyaśca → पापेभ्यः + च

    visarga before c

    -aḥ before c → -aś.

  • 10.1.12 बृहस्पतिस्सविता bṛhaspatissavitā → बृहस्पतिः + सविता

    visarga before s

    Written with a doubled s in this text.

  • 10.1.12 नमोऽग्नयेऽप्सुमते namo'gnaye'psumate → नमः + अग्नये + अप्सुमते

    aḥ before a; e before a

    namaḥ + agnaye → namo ’gnaye; agnaye + apsumate → agnaye ’psumate (P. 6.1.109 (एङः पदान्तादति)).

  • 10.1.12 नम इन्द्राय nama indrāya → नमः + इन्द्राय

    aḥ before a vowel

    namaḥ before i → nama indrāya.

  • 10.1.13 यदपां yadapāṃ → यत् + अपाम्

    t voiced before a vowel

    yat + apām → yad apām.

  • 10.1.13 अत्याशनादतीपानाद्यच्च उग्रात् atyāśanādatīpānādyacca ugrāt → अत्याशनात् + अतीपानात् + यत् + च + उग्रात्

    t voiced; t before c; Vedic hiatus

    -āt before a vowel → -ād; yat before c → yac ca; ca and ugrāt are not joined (see the exceptions).

  • 10.1.13 ह्यवमर्शतु hyavamarśatu → हि + अवमर्शतु

    i before a vowel

    hi + ava- → hy ava-.

  • 10.1.13 सोऽहमपापो so'hamapāpo → सः + अहम् + अपापः

    saḥ before a; m before a vowel

    saḥ + aham → so ’ham; aham apāpaḥ.

  • 10.1.13 गच्छेद्ब्रह्म gacchedbrahma → गच्छेत् + ब्रह्मसलोकताम्

    t voiced before b

    gacchet + brahma- → gacched brahma-.

  • 10.1.13 यश्चाप्सु yaścāpsu → यः + च + अप्सु

    visarga before c; vowel fusion

    yaḥ before c → yaś; ca + apsu → cāpsu.

  • 10.1.13 वरुणस्स पुनात्वघमर्षणः varuṇassa punātvaghamarṣaṇaḥ → वरुणः + सः + पुनातु + अघमर्षणः

    visarga before s; u before a vowel

    varuṇaḥ before s is written with a doubled s; punātu + agha- → punātv agha-.

  • 10.1.13 शृणुह्या śṛṇuhyā → शृणुहि + आ

    i before a vowel

    i + ā → yā: śṛṇuhi + ā → śṛṇuhy ā.

  • 10.1.13 चाभीद्धात्तपसोऽध्यजायत cābhīddhāttapaso'dhyajāyata → च + अभीद्धात् + तपसः + अधि + अजायत

    vowel fusion; t kept before t; aḥ before a; i before a

    ca + abhīddhāt → cābhīddhāt; abhīddhāt tapaso; tapasaḥ + adhi → tapaso ’dhi; adhi + ajāyata → adhy ajāyata.

  • 10.1.14 समुद्रादर्णवादधि samudrādarṇavādadhi → समुद्रात् + अर्णवात् + अधि

    t voiced before a vowel

    -āt before a vowel → -ād (samudrād arṇavād adhi).

  • 10.1.14 विदधद्विश्वस्य vidadhadviśvasya → विदधत् + विश्वस्य

    t voiced before v

    vidadhat + viśvasya → vidadhad viśvasya.

  • 10.1.14 मिषतो वशी miṣato vaśī → मिषतः + वशी

    aḥ before a voiced sound

    miṣataḥ before v → miṣato.

  • 10.1.14 चान्तरिक्षमथो cāntarikṣamatho → च + अन्तरिक्षम् + अथो

    vowel fusion; m before a vowel

    ca + antarikṣam → cāntarikṣam; m joins the vowel.

  • 10.1.14 रजस्स्वमान्तरिक्षे rajassvamāntarikṣe → रजः + स्वम् + आन्तरिक्षे

    visarga before s; m before a vowel

    rajaḥ before s is written with a doubled s; svam āntarikṣe.

  • 10.1.14 इमाग्स्तदापो imāgstadāpo → इमान् + तत् + आपः

    n before t, Taittirīya spelling; t voiced

    imān before t becomes imāṃs; the recitation text writes the anusvāra as ग्: इमाग्स्त; tat āpaḥ → tad āpaḥ.

  • 10.1.14 मृत्योर्हिरण्मयम् mṛtyorhiraṇmayam → मृत्योः + हिरण्मयम्

    visarga after o before a voiced sound

    -oḥ before h becomes -or: mṛtyor hiraṇmayam.

  • 10.1.15 स नस्सुवः sa nassuvaḥ → सः + नः + सुवः

    saḥ before a consonant; visarga before s

    saḥ loses the visarga before n; naḥ before s is written with a doubled s.

  • 10.1.15 ज्योतिरहमस्मि jyotirahamasmi → ज्योतिः + अहम् + अस्मि

    visarga after i before a vowel; m before a vowel

    jyotiḥ + aham → jyotir aham; aham asmi.

  • 10.1.15 ब्रह्माहमस्मि brahmāhamasmi → ब्रह्म + अहम् + अस्मि

    vowel fusion

    a + a → ā: brahma + aham → brahmāham.

  • 10.1.15 योऽहमस्मि yo'hamasmi → यः + अहम् + अस्मि

    aḥ before a

    yaḥ + aham → yo ’ham (P. 6.1.109 (एङः पदान्तादति)).

  • 10.1.15 अहमेवाहं ahamevāhaṃ → अहम् + एव + अहम्

    m before a vowel; vowel fusion

    aham eva; eva + aham → evāham.

  • 10.1.15 अकार्यकार्यवकीर्णी akāryakāryavakīrṇī → अकार्यकारी + अवकीर्णी

    ī before a vowel

    ī + a → ya: akāryakārī + avakīrṇī → akāryakāry avakīrṇī.

  • 10.1.15 वरुणोऽपामघमर्षणस्तस्मात् varuṇo'pāmaghamarṣaṇastasmāt → वरुणः + अपाम् + अघमर्षणः + तस्मात्

    aḥ before a; visarga before t

    varuṇo ’pām; aghamarṣaṇaḥ before t → aghamarṣaṇas tasmāt.

  • 10.1.15 रजोभूमिस्त्व rajobhūmistva → रजोभूमिः + त्व

    visarga before t

    rajobhūmiḥ + tva → rajobhūmis tva.

  • 10.1.15 आक्रान्त्समुद्रः ākrāntsamudraḥ → आक्रान् + समुद्रः

    n before s, t inserted

    A t may be inserted between final n and s, P. 8.3.30 (नश्च): ākrānt samudraḥ.

  • 10.1.15 विधर्मञ्जनयन्प्रजा vidharmañjanayanprajā → विधर्मन् + जनयन् + प्रजाः

    n before j; āḥ before a voiced sound

    vidharman + janayan → vidharmañ janayan; prajāḥ before bh → prajā.

  • 10.1.15 सुवान इन्दुः suvāna induḥ → सुवानः + इन्दुः

    aḥ before a vowel

    suvānaḥ before i loses the visarga: suvāna induḥ.

Junctions outside the general rules

  • 10.1.1 गर्भे · अन्तः — vedic-prakṛtibhāva: गर्भे अन्तः: e before a is left unjoined, as the Vedic text prints it (classical गर्भेऽन्तः); it keeps the syllable count of the pāda.
  • 10.1.1 आ · इदम् — vedic-prakṛtibhāva: भव्यमा इदम्: the particle ā is not fused with the following i (classical भव्यमेदम्); the Vedic text keeps the two apart.
  • 10.1.2 प्रदुघे · उभे — vedic-prakṛtibhāva: प्रदुघे उभे: the final e of the verb is not joined to the following u, as the Vedic text prints it.
  • 10.1.2 उभे · इमे — pragṛhya: उभे इमे: a dual ending in e is pragṛhya and does not join a following vowel (P. 1.1.11 (ईदूदेद्द्विवचनं प्रगृह्यम्)).
  • 10.1.2 इमे · अन्तरिक्षम् — pragṛhya: इमे अन्तरिक्षम्: the dual इमे is pragṛhya (P. 1.1.11 (ईदूदेद्द्विवचनं प्रगृह्यम्)).
  • 10.1.3 गर्भे · अन्तः — vedic-prakṛtibhāva: गर्भे अन्तः: e before a left unjoined, as the Vedic text prints it (the commentator writes गर्भेऽन्तः in his gloss).
  • 10.1.3 वोचे · अमृतम् — vedic-prakṛtibhāva: वोचे अमृतम्: the final e of the verb is not joined to the following a.
  • 10.1.7 नः · आदित्यः — vedic-prakṛtibhāva: तन्नो आदित्यः: naḥ before ā is written no and not joined, as the Vedic text prints it (classical न आदित्यः).
  • 10.1.7 नः · अग्निः — vedic-prakṛtibhāva: तन्नो अग्निः: naḥ before a is written no and the a is not elided, as the Vedic text prints it (classical नोऽग्निः).
  • 10.1.9 नः · अभयम् — vedic-prakṛtibhāva: नो अभयम्: naḥ before a is printed no and the a is not elided, as the Vedic text prints it (classical नोऽभयम्).
  • 10.1.9 तार्क्ष्यः · अरिष्टनेमिः — vedic-prakṛtibhāva: तार्क्ष्यो अरिष्टनेमिः: -aḥ before a printed -o with the a kept, as in the Ṛgveda verse.
  • 10.1.13 च · उग्रात् — vedic-prakṛtibhāva: यच्च उग्रात्: ca and ugrāt are printed unjoined (classical चोग्रात्).
  • 10.1.13 समुद्रः · अर्णवः — vedic-prakṛtibhāva: समुद्रो अर्णवः: -aḥ before a is printed -o and the a is kept (classical समुद्रोऽर्णवः), as the Vedic text prints it.
  • 10.1.14 संवत्सरः · अजायत — vedic-prakṛtibhāva: संवत्सरो अजायत: -aḥ before a printed -o with the a kept (classical संवत्सरोऽजायत), as the Vedic text prints it.
  • 10.1.15 पवित्रे · अधि — vedic-prakṛtibhāva: पवित्रे अधि: e before a left unjoined, as the Vedic text prints it.
  • 10.1.15 सानो · अव्ये — vedic-prakṛtibhāva: सानो अव्ये: the Vedic locative sāno before a is not joined.

Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • 10.1.1 समनुप्रविष्टः samanupraviṣṭaḥ

    a past participle with three preverbs (not a nominal compound) · सम् + अनु + प्र + प्रविष्ट

    Vigraha सम्यग् अनुप्रविष्टः

    having fully entered

  • 10.1.1 प्रजापतिः prajāpatiḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī-tatpuruṣa · प्रजा + पति

    Vigraha प्रजानां पतिः

    the lord of creatures

  • 10.1.1 चराचराणि carācarāṇi

    dvandva · चर + अचर

    Vigraha चराणि च अचराणि च

    moving and unmoving

  • 10.1.1 अव्यक्तम् avyaktam

    nañ-tatpuruṣa · अ + व्यक्त

    Vigraha न व्यक्तम्

    unmanifest

  • 10.1.1 अनन्तरूपम् anantarūpam

    bahuvrīhi · अनन्त + रूप

    Vigraha अनन्तं रूपं यस्य तत्

    having endless form

  • 10.1.2 इष्टापूर्तम् iṣṭāpūrtam

    samāhāra-dvandva (the first member lengthened, Vedic) · इष्ट + पूर्त

    Vigraha इष्टं च पूर्तं च

    what is sacrificed and what is given for the common good

  • 10.1.2 प्रजापतिः prajāpatiḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī-tatpuruṣa · प्रजा + पति

    Vigraha प्रजानां पतिः

    the lord of creatures

  • 10.1.2 अहोरात्राः ahorātrāḥ

    dvandva · अहन् + रात्रि

    Vigraha अहश्च रात्रिश्च

    day and night

  • 10.1.2 अर्धमासाः ardhamāsāḥ

    tatpuruṣa (ekadeśin) · अर्ध + मास

    Vigraha मासस्य अर्धम्

    half-month

  • 10.1.3 हिरण्यगर्भः hiraṇyagarbhaḥ

    bahuvrīhi (or karmadhāraya) · हिरण्य + गर्भ

    Vigraha हिरण्मयो गर्भो यस्य सः

    the golden embryo

  • 10.1.3 विश्वतोमुखः viśvatomukhaḥ

    bahuvrīhi · विश्वतस् + मुख

    Vigraha विश्वतो मुखानि यस्य सः

    with faces on every side

  • 10.1.3 विश्वतश्चक्षुः viśvataścakṣuḥ

    bahuvrīhi · विश्वतस् + चक्षुस्

    Vigraha विश्वतश्चक्षूंषि यस्य सः

    with eyes on every side

  • 10.1.3 विश्वतोहस्तः viśvatohastaḥ

    bahuvrīhi · विश्वतस् + हस्त

    Vigraha विश्वतो हस्ता यस्य सः

    with hands on every side

  • 10.1.3 विश्वतस्पात् viśvataspāt

    bahuvrīhi · विश्वतस् + पाद्

    Vigraha विश्वतः पादा यस्य सः

    with feet on every side

  • 10.1.3 द्यावापृथिवी dyāvāpṛthivī

    devatā-dvandva (Vedic) · दिव् + पृथिवी

    Vigraha द्यौश्च पृथिवी च

    heaven and earth

  • 10.1.3 एकनीळम् ekanīḻam

    karmadhāraya · एक + नीड

    Vigraha एकं नीडम्

    one nest

  • 10.1.4 द्यावापृथिवी dyāvāpṛthivī

    devatā-dvandva (Vedic) · दिव् + पृथिवी

    Vigraha द्यौश्च पृथिवी च

    heaven and earth

  • 10.1.4 प्रथमजाः prathamajāḥ

    upapada-tatpuruṣa · प्रथम + जा

    Vigraha प्रथमं जातः

    first-born

  • 10.1.4 सदसस्पतिम् sadasaspatim

    ṣaṣṭhī-tatpuruṣa with the genitive kept (aluk), Vedic · सदस् + पति

    Vigraha सदसः पतिः

    the lord of the seat (assembly)

  • 10.1.5 सहस्राक्षस्य sahasrākṣasya

    bahuvrīhi · सहस्र + अक्षि

    Vigraha सहस्रम् अक्षीणि यस्य सः

    thousand-eyed

  • 10.1.5 महादेवस्य mahādevasya

    karmadhāraya · महत् + देव

    Vigraha महान् देवः

    the great god

  • 10.1.5 वक्रतुण्डाय vakratuṇḍāya

    bahuvrīhi · वक्र + तुण्ड

    Vigraha वक्रं तुण्डं यस्य सः

    having a curved trunk

  • 10.1.5 चक्रतुण्डाय cakratuṇḍāya

    bahuvrīhi · चक्र + तुण्ड

    Vigraha चक्रमिव तुण्डं यस्य सः

    having a mouth like a discus

  • 10.1.6 महासेनाय mahāsenāya

    bahuvrīhi · महत् + सेना

    Vigraha महती सेना यस्य सः

    having a great army

  • 10.1.6 षण्मुखः ṣaṇmukhaḥ

    bahuvrīhi · षष् + मुख

    Vigraha षट् मुखानि यस्य सः

    six-faced

  • 10.1.6 सुवर्णपक्षाय suvarṇapakṣāya

    bahuvrīhi · सुवर्ण + पक्ष

    Vigraha सुवर्णमयौ पक्षौ यस्य सः

    golden-winged

  • 10.1.6 वेदात्मनाय vedātmanāya

    bahuvrīhi · वेद + आत्मन्

    Vigraha वेद आत्मा यस्य सः

    whose self is the Veda

  • 10.1.6 हिरण्यगर्भाय hiraṇyagarbhāya

    bahuvrīhi · हिरण्य + गर्भ

    Vigraha हिरण्मयो गर्भो यस्य सः

    the golden embryo

  • 10.1.6 वज्रनखाय vajranakhāya

    bahuvrīhi · वज्र + नख

    Vigraha वज्रमिव नखा यस्य सः

    with claws like adamant

  • 10.1.6 तीक्ष्णदंष्ट्राय tīkṣṇadaṃṣṭrāya

    bahuvrīhi · तीक्ष्ण + दंष्ट्रा

    Vigraha तीक्ष्णा दंष्ट्रा यस्य सः

    sharp-fanged

  • 10.1.7 महद्युतिकराय mahadyutikarāya

    upapada-tatpuruṣa · महत् + द्युति + कर

    Vigraha महतीं द्युतिं करोतीति

    maker of great light

  • 10.1.7 कन्यकुमारि kanyakumāri

    karmadhāraya (first member shortened) · कन्या + कुमारी

    Vigraha कन्या चासौ कुमारी च

    the maiden Kumārī

  • 10.1.7 सहस्रपरमा sahasraparamā

    pañcamī-tatpuruṣa in sense · सहस्र + परम

    Vigraha सहस्रात् परमा

    higher than a thousand

  • 10.1.7 शतमूला śatamūlā

    bahuvrīhi · शत + मूल

    Vigraha शतं मूलानि यस्याः सा

    hundred-rooted

  • 10.1.7 शताङ्कुरा śatāṅkurā

    bahuvrīhi · शत + अङ्कुर

    Vigraha शतम् अङ्कुरा यस्याः सा

    hundred-shooted

  • 10.1.7 दुस्स्वप्ननाशनी dussvapnanāśanī

    upapada-tatpuruṣa · दुस् + स्वप्न + नाशन

    Vigraha दुःस्वप्नं नाशयतीति

    destroyer of bad dreams

  • 10.1.8 अश्वक्रान्ते aśvakrānte

    tṛtīyā-tatpuruṣa · अश्व + क्रान्त

    Vigraha अश्वैः क्रान्ता

    trodden by horses

  • 10.1.8 रथक्रान्ते rathakrānte

    tṛtīyā-tatpuruṣa · रथ + क्रान्त

    Vigraha रथैः क्रान्ता

    trodden by chariots

  • 10.1.8 विष्णुक्रान्ते viṣṇukrānte

    tṛtīyā-tatpuruṣa · विष्णु + क्रान्त

    Vigraha विष्णुना क्रान्ता

    trodden by Viṣṇu

  • 10.1.8 वसुन्धरा vasundharā

    upapada-tatpuruṣa (aluk, with the nasal) · वसु + धरा

    Vigraha वसूनि धारयतीति

    bearer of wealth

  • 10.1.8 लोकधारिणी lokadhāriṇī

    upapada-tatpuruṣa · लोक + धारिणी

    Vigraha लोकान् धारयतीति

    bearer of the worlds

  • 10.1.8 शतबाहुना śatabāhunā

    bahuvrīhi · शत + बाहु

    Vigraha शतं बाहवो यस्य सः

    hundred-armed

  • 10.1.8 दुष्कृतम् duṣkṛtam

    prādi-tatpuruṣa · दुस् + कृत

    Vigraha दुष्टं कृतम्

    wrongdoing

  • 10.1.8 ब्रह्मदत्ता brahmadattā

    tṛtīyā-tatpuruṣa · ब्रह्मन् + दत्त

    Vigraha ब्रह्मणा दत्ता

    given by Brahmā

  • 10.1.9 स्वस्तिदाः svastidāḥ

    upapada-tatpuruṣa · स्वस्ति + दा

    Vigraha स्वस्ति ददातीति

    giver of well-being

  • 10.1.9 विशस्पतिः viśaspatiḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī-tatpuruṣa with the genitive kept (aluk), Vedic · विश् + पति

    Vigraha विशः पतिः

    lord of the people

  • 10.1.9 वृत्रहा vṛtrahā

    upapada-tatpuruṣa · वृत्र + हन्

    Vigraha वृत्रं हतवान्

    slayer of Vṛtra

  • 10.1.9 अभयङ्करः abhayaṅkaraḥ

    upapada-tatpuruṣa · अभय + कर

    Vigraha अभयं करोतीति

    maker of safety

  • 10.1.9 वृद्धश्रवाः vṛddhaśravāḥ

    bahuvrīhi · वृद्ध + श्रवस्

    Vigraha वृद्धं श्रवो यस्य सः

    of increased fame

  • 10.1.9 विश्ववेदाः viśvavedāḥ

    bahuvrīhi · विश्व + वेदस्

    Vigraha विश्वं वेदो यस्य सः

    possessing all

  • 10.1.9 अरिष्टनेमिः ariṣṭanemiḥ

    bahuvrīhi · अरिष्ट + नेमि

    Vigraha अरिष्टा नेमिर्यस्य सः

    whose wheel-rim is unharmed

  • 10.1.9 आपान्तमन्युः āpāntamanyuḥ

    bahuvrīhi · आपान्त + मन्यु

    Vigraha आपान्तो मन्युर्यस्य सः

    whose anger is quickly roused (meaning uncertain)

  • 10.1.9 तृपलप्रभर्मा tṛpalaprabharmā

    bahuvrīhi · तृपल + प्रभर्मन्

    Vigraha तृपलं प्रभर्म यस्य सः

    swift in striking (meaning uncertain)

  • 10.1.10 गन्धद्वाराम् gandhadvārām

    bahuvrīhi · गन्ध + द्वार

    Vigraha गन्धो द्वारं यस्याः सा

    whose door (sign) is fragrance

  • 10.1.10 दुराधर्षाम् durādharṣām

    prādi-tatpuruṣa · दुर् + आधर्ष

    Vigraha दुःखेन आधर्षितुं शक्या

    hard to assail

  • 10.1.10 नित्यपुष्टाम् nityapuṣṭām

    karmadhāraya · नित्य + पुष्ट

    Vigraha नित्यं पुष्टा

    ever nourished

  • 10.1.10 सर्वभूतानाम् sarvabhūtānām

    karmadhāraya · सर्व + भूत

    Vigraha सर्वाणि भूतानि

    all beings

  • 10.1.10 अलक्ष्मीः alakṣmīḥ

    nañ-tatpuruṣa · अ + लक्ष्मी

    Vigraha न लक्ष्मीः

    misfortune

  • 10.1.10 विष्णुमुखाः viṣṇumukhāḥ

    bahuvrīhi · विष्णु + मुख

    Vigraha विष्णुर्मुखं येषां ते

    headed by Viṣṇu

  • 10.1.10 वज्रबाहुः vajrabāhuḥ

    bahuvrīhi · वज्र + बाहु

    Vigraha वज्रं बाहौ यस्य सः

    thunderbolt-armed

  • 10.1.11 ब्रह्मणस्पते brahmaṇaspate

    ṣaṣṭhī-tatpuruṣa with the genitive kept (aluk) · ब्रह्मन् + पति

    Vigraha ब्रह्मणः पतिः

    lord of brahman

  • 10.1.11 यज्ञशमलम् yajñaśamalam

    ṣaṣṭhī-tatpuruṣa · यज्ञ + शमल

    Vigraha यज्ञस्य शमलम्

    the defilement of the sacrifice

  • 10.1.11 दुष्कृतानि duṣkṛtāni

    prādi-tatpuruṣa · दुस् + कृत

    Vigraha दुष्टानि कृतानि

    wrong deeds

  • 10.1.11 सगणः sagaṇaḥ

    bahuvrīhi (saha-) · स + गण

    Vigraha गणेन सह

    with his troop

  • 10.1.11 वृत्रहन् vṛtrahan

    upapada-tatpuruṣa · वृत्र + हन्

    Vigraha वृत्रं हतवान्

    slayer of Vṛtra

  • 10.1.11 सुमित्राः sumitrāḥ

    bahuvrīhi / prādi · सु + मित्र

    Vigraha शोभनं मित्रं यासां ताः

    good friends

  • 10.1.11 दुर्मित्राः durmitrāḥ

    bahuvrīhi / prādi · दुर् + मित्र

    Vigraha दुष्टं मित्रं यासां ताः

    bad friends

  • 10.1.11 मयोभुवः mayobhuvaḥ

    upapada-tatpuruṣa · मयस् + भू

    Vigraha मयो भावयन्तीति

    bringers of joy

  • 10.1.12 शिवतमः śivatamaḥ

    superlative suffix (taddhita) · शिव + तम

    Vigraha अतिशयेन शिवः

    most auspicious

  • 10.1.12 हिरण्यशृङ्गम् hiraṇyaśṛṅgam

    bahuvrīhi · हिरण्य + शृङ्ग

    Vigraha हिरण्मयं शृङ्गं यस्य सः

    golden-horned

  • 10.1.12 असाधूनाम् asādhūnām

    nañ-tatpuruṣa · अ + साधु

    Vigraha न साधवः

    the bad

  • 10.1.12 दुष्कृतम् duṣkṛtam

    prādi-tatpuruṣa · दुस् + कृत

    Vigraha दुष्टं कृतम्

    wrongdoing

  • 10.1.13 अत्याशनात् atyāśanāt

    prādi-tatpuruṣa · अति + आशन

    Vigraha अतिमात्रम् आशनम्

    over-eating

  • 10.1.13 अतीपानात् atīpānāt

    prādi-tatpuruṣa · अति + पान

    Vigraha अतिमात्रं पानम्

    over-drinking

  • 10.1.13 अपापः apāpaḥ

    bahuvrīhi (nañ) · अ + पाप

    Vigraha न विद्यते पापं यस्य सः

    sinless

  • 10.1.13 विरजः virajaḥ

    bahuvrīhi (prādi) · वि + रजस्

    Vigraha विगतं रजो यस्मात् सः

    stainless

  • 10.1.13 मुक्तकिल्बिषः muktakilbiṣaḥ

    bahuvrīhi · मुक्त + किल्बिष

    Vigraha मुक्तं किल्बिषं येन सः

    freed of guilt

  • 10.1.13 ब्रह्मसलोकताम् brahmasalokatām

    ṣaṣṭhī-tatpuruṣa · ब्रह्मन् + सलोकता

    Vigraha ब्रह्मणः सलोकता

    sharing a world with Brahman

  • 10.1.13 अघमर्षणः aghamarṣaṇaḥ

    upapada-tatpuruṣa · अघ + मर्षण

    Vigraha अघानि मर्षयतीति

    wiper-away of sin

  • 10.1.13 मरुद्वृधे marudvṛdhe

    upapada-tatpuruṣa (a river’s name) · मरुत् + वृधा

    Vigraha मरुद्भिः वर्धिता

    Marudvṛdhā, ‘swollen by the winds’

  • 10.1.14 अहोरात्राणि ahorātrāṇi

    dvandva · अहन् + रात्रि

    Vigraha अहानि च रात्रयश्च

    days and nights

  • 10.1.14 सूर्याचन्द्रमसौ sūryācandramasau

    devatā-dvandva · सूर्य + चन्द्रमस्

    Vigraha सूर्यश्च चन्द्रमाश्च

    sun and moon

  • 10.1.14 यथापूर्वम् yathāpūrvam

    avyayībhāva · यथा + पूर्व

    Vigraha पूर्वम् अनतिक्रम्य

    as before

  • 10.1.14 पुण्यकृताम् puṇyakṛtām

    upapada-tatpuruṣa · पुण्य + कृत्

    Vigraha पुण्यं कुर्वन्तीति

    doers of good

  • 10.1.14 द्यावापृथिव्योः dyāvāpṛthivyoḥ

    devatā-dvandva · दिव् + पृथिवी

    Vigraha द्यौश्च पृथिवी च

    heaven and earth

  • 10.1.15 अकार्यकारी akāryakārī

    upapada-tatpuruṣa · अकार्य + कारिन्

    Vigraha अकार्यं कर्तुं शीलं यस्य सः

    one who does what must not be done

  • 10.1.15 भ्रूणहा bhrūṇahā

    upapada-tatpuruṣa · भ्रूण + हन्

    Vigraha भ्रूणं हन्तीति

    slayer of an embryo

  • 10.1.15 गुरुतल्पगः gurutalpagaḥ

    upapada-tatpuruṣa · गुरु + तल्प + ग

    Vigraha गुरोस्तल्पं गच्छतीति

    violator of the teacher’s bed

  • 10.1.15 अघमर्षणः aghamarṣaṇaḥ

    upapada-tatpuruṣa · अघ + मर्षण

    Vigraha अघानि मर्षयतीति

    remover of sin

  • 10.1.15 रजोभूमिः rajobhūmiḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī-tatpuruṣa · रजस् + भूमि

    Vigraha रजसो भूमिः

    ground of impurity

Morphology

10.1.1 अम्भस्य
A genitive formed as if from an a-stem (classical अम्भसः, from ambhas). The commentator explains it as ‘of the water’.
10.1.1 व्योमन्
Vedic locative without ending (classical व्योम्नि).
10.1.1 व्यचसर्ज
Printed so in the base; the commentator knows another reading, व्यससर्ज, and explains both as ‘created’ (व्यससर्जेति पाठेऽपि तथैव व्याख्येयम्).
10.1.1 प्रसूती
A Vedic feminine with long ī (classical प्रसूतिः); the commentator glosses it सृष्टिरुत्पत्तिः.
10.1.1 अणीयसम्
The comparative of aṇu treated as an a-stem (classical अणीयः).
10.1.2 ईशे
A Vedic present middle 3rd singular without the -t- of classical ईष्टे; the commentator glosses नेष्टे.
10.1.2 परिजग्रभत्
A reduplicated aorist of the Vedic root grabh (classical grah); with न it denies that anyone has grasped him above, across or in the middle.
10.1.2 प्रदुघे
Perfect 3rd singular middle of pra-√duh, ‘he milked out’ (the commentator: दुग्धवान्): he drew from the waters, and from the two regions, all that creatures need.
10.1.2 सुवः
The Taittirīya form of svar, ‘heaven’, the third of the three worlds (bhūḥ, bhuvaḥ, suvaḥ).
10.1.3 संदृशे
A Vedic dative infinitive, ‘for seeing’: his form does not stand within sight.
10.1.3 मनीषा
Read by the commentator as ‘the ruler of minds’ (from manas and √īś), qualifying the inward mind that grasps him; it can also be read as the instrumental of manīṣā, ‘by thought’.
10.1.3 अष्टौ
With इति, ‘[the] eight [verses beginning] …’: a pratīka reference, not text spoken in full here. The commentator identifies the eight verses as the hymn ‘hiraṇyagarbhaḥ samavartatāgre’ in the fourth kāṇḍa of the Saṃhitā, and अद्भ्यस्सम्भूतः as the opening of an anuvāka of the Cāturhotra chapter.
10.1.3 प्रत्यङ्मुखाः
Printed as one word; the commentator divides it, pratyaṅ ‘the inner one’ and mukhāḥ ‘the chief’. Read as one word it means ‘facing back (inward)’.
10.1.3 वोचे
A Vedic aorist of √vac with pra separated from it; the commentator glosses प्रोवाच, ‘proclaimed’.
10.1.3 एकनीळम्
The Vedic ḷ stands for ḍ between vowels (नीड, ‘nest’).
10.1.4 पदा
Vedic neuter plural in -ā (classical पदानि); so also निहिता and विश्वा.
10.1.4 सत्
The present participle of √as standing with pitā, ‘being the father’; the commentator glosses सज्जनको भवति.
10.1.4 आनशानाः
A Vedic perfect participle middle of √aś, ‘to reach, obtain’.
10.1.4 अभ्यैरयन्त
abhi + ā + airayanta (imperfect of √īr): ‘they went towards’.
10.1.4 अयासिषम्
s-aorist 1st singular of √yā, ‘I have come to’; the commentator: अयासिषमहं प्राप्तवानस्मि, and the past tense signals that the attainment is sure (शीघ्रं प्राप्तवानित्यनया विवक्षया).
10.1.5 पुरुषाय
In these gāyatrīs a dative or genitive stands where an accusative is meant (‘we know the Person’); the commentator states the rule for all of them: सर्वत्र गायत्रीमन्त्रेषु बहुलं लिङ्गादिव्यत्ययः.
10.1.5 प्रचोदयात्
A Vedic optative/injunctive of the causative of pra-√cud; the commentator glosses प्रचोदयतु, ‘may he impel’.
10.1.5 दन्तिः
The stem dantin treated as an i-stem (classical दन्ती).
10.1.5 आगहि
A Vedic aorist imperative of ā-√gam (classical आगच्छ).
10.1.5 विद्म
Perfect 1st plural of √vid with present sense; the Tirupati e-text reads विद्महे here, as in the following gāyatrīs.
10.1.6 वेदात्मनाय
The n-stem ātman treated as an a-stem; the commentator: वर्णव्यत्ययेन वेदात्मकाय.
10.1.6 षण्मुखः
The base reads षण्मुखः; the Ānandāśrama pariśiṣṭa text and the Tirupati e-text agree on नन्दिः here, a difference the edition lists for review. The anonymous commentary explains a ṣaṇmukha gāyatrī at this point.
10.1.7 लालीलाय
An obscure word. The commentator derives it as one whose sport (helā, līlā) is sport among the gods, and mentions another way of dividing the line (vaiśvānarāya vidma helālīlāya); the meaning is uncertain.
10.1.7 दुर्गिः
The commentator: the i-ending is Vedic, इकारान्तत्वं छान्दसम्.
10.1.7 कात्यायनाय
The commentator takes Kātyāyana as the goddess’s father and the dative as ‘for Kātyāyana’s (daughter)’: कात्यायनाय स्वपित्रे.
10.1.7 दुस्स्वप्ननाशनी
The base prints नाशनी; the Ānandāśrama pariśiṣṭa text and the Tirupati e-text read नाशिनी (listed for review).
10.1.8 एवा
Vedic lengthened form of eva, ‘thus’; the commentator: एवमित्यव्ययविभक्तेराङादेशश्छान्दसः.
10.1.8 वसुन्धरा
Nominative used as a vocative in a line of vocatives.
10.1.8 इष्टके
The commentator derives iṣṭakā here as ‘she who is praised by chosen mantras’ (इष्टैर्मन्त्रैर्भक्तैः कायते शब्द्यते स्तूयत इतीष्टका); the same word elsewhere means a sacrificial brick.
10.1.9 भयामहे
Vedic present middle of √bhī (classical बिभीमः).
10.1.9 कृधि
Vedic aorist imperative of √kṛ (classical कुरु); the commentator glosses कृधि कुरु.
10.1.9 शग्धि
Vedic aorist imperative of √śak, ‘be able (to help)’; the commentator explains it differently, as ‘destroy’ (शग्धि शातय विनाशयेति).
10.1.9 आपान्तमन्युः
This line and the next are a Ṛgvedic verse on Soma and Indra. Its first epithets are obscure; the base prints आपान्त and मन्युः apart, and the commentator’s explanation of them is damaged in the copy used.
10.1.9 अर्वाक्
The commentator takes the singular for a plural: अर्वाग्वचनव्यत्ययः। अर्वाञ्चि.
10.1.10 जज्ञानम्
Perfect participle middle of √jan; the commentator gives its formation: जनेः शानचि शपः श्लौ सति जज्ञानमिति रूपम्.
10.1.10 आवः
Aorist of √vṛ (‘uncover’) with vi separated from it; the commentator: व्यवहिताश्च — preverb and verb may stand apart.
10.1.10 नृक्षरा
The base prints भवा नृक्षरा and the commentator explains nṛkṣarā, ‘bringing forth men’. The Ṛgveda verse (RV 1.22.15) reads भवानृक्षरा, which its tradition divides bhava anṛkṣarā, ‘thornless’; the edition records both.
10.1.10 यच्छा
Vedic lengthening of the final vowel; the commentator: छान्दसं दीर्घत्वम्.
10.1.10 षोडशी
The commentator gives two readings: the moon of sixteen digits (Indra like the full moon), or one who has the sixteen-syllabled Śrīvidyā.
10.1.11 ष्ठा
The verb is stha, ‘you are’, retroflexed after hi and lengthened in the Vedic text; the commentator divides the words as hi and stha. The commentator places these mantras in the bath, said standing navel-deep in the water (नाभिद्वयसजले स्थित्वा जप्यान्मन्त्रानाह).
10.1.11 कृणुहि
A Vedic imperative of √kṛ from the stem kṛṇu- (classical कुरु).
10.1.11 दधातन
Vedic 2nd plural imperative ending -tana (classical धत्त).
10.1.11 औशिजम्
The accusative is attracted to kakṣīvantam; the commentator notes the case-exchange (विभक्तिव्यत्ययः) and names Uśij as the mother.
10.1.12 भाजयत
Causative imperative of √bhaj, ‘give a share’; with the genitive tasya, ‘give us a share of that’.
10.1.12 जनयथा
Vedic lengthened ending; the commentator: ‘make us able to beget children’.
10.1.12 क्षयाय
kṣaya means both ‘dwelling’ and ‘destruction’; the commentator takes it here as the destruction of the net of sin (पापजालस्य क्षयाय विप्रणाशाय).
10.1.13 अपगच्छतात्
The imperative in -tāt, used of wishes.
10.1.13 ब्रह्मसलोकताम्
The base prints ब्रह्म and सलोकताम् apart; read as one compound, ‘the state of sharing a world with Brahman (Brahmā)’.
10.1.13 सचता
The commentator: the preverb ā stands apart from sacata and the vowel is lengthened, both Vedic; he reads the second person plural, ‘attend (to my praise)’.
10.1.13 अभीद्धात्
The commentator reads abhīddha as the all-shining Supreme (सर्वतो देदीप्यामानः परमात्मा) and tapas as reflection, from which right order and truth were born. These three verses are the aghamarṣaṇa, said while immersed in the water (जले निमग्नस्य प्राणायामार्थमघमर्षणसूक्तमाह).
10.1.14 आन्तरिक्षे
The base prints the long ā (āntarikṣe); the commentator glosses आन्तरिक्षे सर्व as ‘all through the mid-region’.
10.1.14 विरोदसी
An obscure form; the commentator takes it as ‘in heaven and earth’ (विरोदसी द्यावापृथिव्योर्यत्पापमस्ति).
10.1.14 अघमर्षणः
The first two occurrences name Varuṇa as the remover of sin; the third, after the Vasus and Varuṇa, the commentator takes as the seer Aghamarṣaṇa (अघमर्षणनामर्षिः पुनातु).
10.1.15 संशिशाधि
A Vedic reduplicated imperative of sam-√śās; the anonymous commentator glosses it as ‘favour (us) fully’, with heaven as what is granted.
10.1.15 जुहोमि
The commentator explains the sip of water after the bath as an offering: मद्देहवर्तिभ्यः प्राणाद्यभिमानिभ्यो देवेभ्यो जलरूपं हविर्दत्तमित्यर्थः.
10.1.15 भ्रूणहा
The commentator gives two senses of bhrūṇa: a brahmin learned in the Veda and its limbs, or an embryo (वेदवेदाङ्गविद्ब्राह्मणो गर्भो वा).
10.1.15 त्व
The commentator: त्वम् with its anusvāra lost (Vedic), and ‘not’ is to be supplied before rodayasva: त्वं मां न रोदयस्व। नकारोऽध्याहर्तव्यः.
10.1.15 आक्रान्
Vedic aorist of √kram; the base prints it with long ā. The commentator glosses ‘has pervaded’.
10.1.15 सोमः
The commentator gives a second, Śaiva reading of soma in this verse: ‘with Umā’ (sa + umā), Umā standing for the knowledge of brahman (तयोमया सह विद्यमानत्वात्सोमः).

Anvaya

Prose already in prose order; no anvaya is given.

Syntax

The anuvāka is a chain of separate mantras, not one sentence. Units 10.1.1–10.1.4 are hymn verses about the highest in the third person, built on relative clauses (yasmin … yena … yataḥ … yat) and on refrains (tad eva … tad u …; na … na …). Two pratīkas, ‘adbhyaḥ sambhūtaḥ’ and ‘hiraṇyagarbha ityaṣṭau’, stand for passages recited in full at that point. 10.1.4–10.1.5 turn to Agni with imperatives (uddīpyasva, āvaha, mā hiṃsīt). The twelve gāyatrīs share one frame: X vidmahe, Y dhīmahi, tan no Z pracodayāt — ‘we know X, we meditate on Y; may Z impel us to that’, the names standing in the dative where the sense is accusative. The bath mantras (from 10.1.7) address dūrvā, the earth, Indra, the waters and Varuṇa in the vocative with imperatives (haratu, hana, dehi, nuda, kṛdhi, punantu), and close in the first person (aham asmi, juhomi).

Ambiguities

prasūtā jagataḥ prasūtī (10.1.1)
The base reads prasūtā … prasūtī; the anonymous commentator reads the second word as prasūtiḥ, ‘the birth of the world’, with prasūtā agreeing with it. The padaccheda follows him. His gloss also allows the reading vyasasarja for vyacasarja, explained the same way.
pratyaṅmukhāḥ tiṣṭhati (10.1.3)
Printed as one plural word, ‘facing inward’, beside a singular verb. The anonymous commentator divides it: pratyaṅ, ‘the inner one’, within the sheaths of food and the rest (प्रत्यङ्ङन्नमयादिकोशेभ्य आन्तरः), and mukhāḥ, ‘the chief’, as overseer of body and senses. The padaccheda keeps the printed word.
bhavā nṛkṣarā or bhavānṛkṣarā (10.1.10)
The Ṛgveda verse (RV 1.22.15) is usually divided bhava anṛkṣarā, ‘without thorns’; the anonymous commentator divides bhavā nṛkṣarā and explains nṛkṣarā from nṛ, ‘people’. The padaccheda follows the commentator and says so.
lālīlāya (10.1.7)
An epithet of Agni Vaiśvānara found nowhere else in the sources, and the witnesses differ near it. The anonymous commentator takes it as helā-līla, ‘whose sport is play’, the h lost, and also offers the division vaiśvānarāya vidma helālīlāya; both are his suggestions.
tva māṃ rodayasva (10.1.15)
As printed the line says ‘make me weep’. The anonymous commentator reads tva as tvam and supplies a negative, ‘do not make me weep’; the translation marks the supplied word.
soma (10.1.15)
The closing Ṛgvedic verse speaks of Soma pressed on the woollen filter. The anonymous commentator also reads soma as sa-umā, ‘with Umā’, and the ridge as the lotus of the heart; that is his reading, not the verse’s plain sense.

Metre

mixed · syllables counted as written: 22 + 23 + 22 + 21 + 25 + 21 + 22 + 22 + 22 + 22 + 21 + 24 + 15 + 19 + 16 + 15 + 17 + 18 + 16 + 15 + 22 + 23 + 13 + 22 + 24 + 23 + 23 + 23 + 20 + 21 + 20 + 22 + 21 + 23 + 22 + 22 + 21 + 15 + 8 + 15 + 16 + 16 + 16 + 19 + 8 + 16 + 8 + 16 + 8 + 16 + 8 + 16 + 9 + 17 + 9 + 17 + 8 + 16 + 8 + 16 + 10 + 17 + 9 + 15 + 8 + 16 + 8 + 16 + 16 + 16 + 16 + 16 + 15 + 16 + 16 + 12 + 16 + 16 + 16 + 16 + 17 + 16 + 16 + 20 + 15 + 16 + 18 + 20 + 22 + 21 + 22 + 22 + 14 + 8 + 16 + 16 + 5 + 7 + 23 + 16 + 18 + 14 + 8 + 20 + 23 + 22 + 22 + 21 + 30 + 16 + 8 + 16 + 8 + 16 + 8 + 19 + 17 + 16 + 22 + 26 + 19 + 16 + 15 + 16 + 16 + 14 + 24 + 22 + 15 + 16 + 16 + 16 + 16 + 14 + 15 + 15 + 19 + 13 + 16 + 14 + 8 + 11 + 10 + 9 + 9 + 11 + 18 + 17 + 16 + 22 + 23

Several metres in one anuvāka. 10.1.1–10.1.4 are mostly triṣṭubh lines printed two pādas to a line, counted 20–25 syllables (10.1.1: 22, 23, 22, 21, 25, 21, 22, 22, 22, 22), with the pratīka line in 10.1.3 (13) outside the metre and anuṣṭubh lines in 10.1.2 (15–19). The gāyatrīs are 8 + 8 + 8 in principle but are printed across lines and vary (16 and 8, 17 and 9). The bath mantras are mainly anuṣṭubh lines of 16; the anonymous commentator calls the verse mṛttike hana … a six-pāda mahāpaṅkti (षट्पादेयमृङ्महापङ्क्तिच्छन्दस्का), where Bhāskara counts mṛttike hana as an anuṣṭubh (मृत्तिके हनेति तृतीया अनुष्टुप्). The Ṛgvedic verses in 10.1.9–10.1.11 and 10.1.15 are triṣṭubh (20–23); 10.1.15 opens with short prose formulas (8–11).

Textual variations between witnesses

  • Variant reading Ānandāśrama pariśiṣṭa text (1927): हरिःओमोमंभस्यप — The Ānandāśrama pariśiṣṭa text reads हरिःओमोम for अ (letters as collated); the base and the Tirupati e-text agree against it.
  • Witness error Ānandāśrama pariśiṣṭa text (1927): येनाऽऽदित्यपति तेजसा — The Ānandāśrama pariśiṣṭa text alone reads — for स्त; the base and the Tirupati e-text agree.
  • Witness error Ānandāśrama pariśiṣṭa text (1927): यतः प्रसृता जगतः — The Ānandāśrama pariśiṣṭa text alone reads ृ for ू; the base and the Tirupati e-text agree.
  • Witness error Ānandāśrama pariśiṣṭa text (1927): इष्टापूर्वं बहुधा — The Ānandāśrama pariśiṣṭa text alone reads व for त; the base and the Tirupati e-text agree.
  • Variant reading Ānandāśrama pariśiṣṭa text (1927): तदेवाग्निस्तद्वायुस्तदु चन्द्रमाः। — The Ānandāśrama pariśiṣṭa text omits त्सूर्यस्त (letters as collated); the base and the Tirupati e-text agree against it.

11 Connections with other scriptures

  • Kaṭha Upaniṣad 2.3.9 Direct textual parallel

    न सन्दृशे तिष्ठति रूपमस्य न चक्षुषा पश्यति कश्चनैनम्

    na sandṛśe tiṣṭhati rūpamasya na cakṣuṣā paśyati kaścanainam

    The first verse of 10.1.3 is the Kaṭha’s verse almost word for word; the Kaṭha reads ya etad vidur where the Mahānārāyaṇa reads ya enaṃ viduḥ.

  • Bṛhadāraṇyaka Upaniṣad 3.8.11 Doctrinal comparison

    एतस्मिन्नु खल्वक्षरे गार्ग्याकाश ओतश्च

    etasminnu khalvakṣare gārgyākāśa otaśca

    The anonymous commentator cites the Gārgī dialogue on akṣare parame vyoman (10.1.1): the unmanifest, on which the world is woven, rests in the imperishable. 10.1.3 uses the same pair of words, sa otaḥ protaś ca.

  • Bṛhadāraṇyaka Upaniṣad 1.4.10 Doctrinal comparison

    अहं ब्रह्मास्मीति

    ahaṃ brahmāsmīti

    The bather’s brahmāham asmi (10.1.15) repeats the Bṛhadāraṇyaka’s words. The anonymous commentator and Sāyaṇa read it there as the recognition of what one already is, not a new state gained.

  • Ṛgveda 1.22.15 Direct textual parallel

    स्योना पृथिवि

    syonā pṛthivi

    The Ṛgveda verse to the earth quoted in 10.1.10; the Ṛgveda text joins bhavānṛkṣarā, which the anonymous commentator divides bhavā nṛkṣarā.

  • Ṛgveda 1.89.6 Direct textual parallel

    स्वस्ति न इन्द्रो वृद्धश्रवाः

    svasti na indro vṛddhaśravāḥ

    The svasti verse in 10.1.9 is this Ṛgveda verse; the Taittirīya text writes the sibilant doubled before s (vṛddhaśravās svasti).

Every quoted phrase is checked against the text of the passage cited (sources in section 14).

12 Contemporary relevance
Contemporary Reflection

Theme Ritual and understanding

The anuvāka sets a vast hymn about the source of everything beside the plain steps of washing: grass, earth, knee-deep water, a sip from the hand. The commentators hold the two together: the bath is where the hymn is put into practice, and its last words are ‘I am brahman’. One modern way to read this is that large ideas need small, repeated acts to live in, and small acts need a large idea to be more than habit. This is a modern reflection on the passage, not part of its traditional meaning.

13 Reflect and contemplate

A question to begin The hymn says the highest is seen not by the eye but by the heart. What in your own experience is known that way?

Go deeper

A philosophical inquiry Why might a text place a hymn to the source of all things directly before the mantras of an ordinary daily bath?

A contemplation exercise The next time you wash, do it slowly and attentively, as a thing done on purpose rather than in a hurry.

An invitation to attend, not a treatment; no medical or psychological claim is made.
